# PART BT — FAT TAILS AND THE AXIS THAT MEASURES THEM (2026-08-12)

## BT.0 — Scope
Two threads: (1) the fat-tail question — distributions, human vs AI, why the estate's whole architecture is a tail instrument; (2) the `affect` build report — verification, engineering wins, and two gate deviations the register must name honestly.

## BT.1 — Fat tails, human vs AI (doctrine answer)
- **Thin vs fat tails:** in thin-tailed (Gaussian-ish) worlds, extremes are negligible and averages describe reality. In fat-tailed worlds, rare extreme events carry most of the total risk — the tail *is* the story.
- **Human performance:** within a domain, roughly bounded and bell-shaped; a professional's worst day is capped by fatigue, malpractice frames, and one-at-a-time action.
- **AI behavior: the jagged frontier + the correlated tail.** Capability is spiky across adjacent tasks (superhuman here, broken one step over), and — the decisive difference — **the tail is correlated at scale**: one bad behavior executes millions of times simultaneously. A human's bad hour harms one room; a model's bad sycophancy spiral harms every grieving user who triggers it, tonight.
- **The measurement consequence (canonized):** average-score benchmarks measure the middle of the distribution; **harm lives in the tail.** A model that is 99% fine and 1% catastrophically manipulative has an excellent average and an unacceptable tail. GSPC's tail instruments: per-item rows (tails visible, not averaged away), UNMEASURED (refusing to fake tail knowledge), worst-category reporting, the Blind-Spot Rule (fleet-wide failure = shared tail exposure), and sims (BR outcome chains) which exist to *sample the tail cheaply and safely* before reality does.
- **The money translation:** insurers price tails, not averages (BE gold: Munich Re aiSure, Armilla/Chaucer, AIUC). A measurement layer that quantifies tail behavior is exactly what the insurance market cannot build for itself — per-item, law-anchored, signed. That is the honey-to-money path for tail data specifically.
- **THEORY, buildable:** derived tail metrics from per-item rows already collected — tail-failure rate at threshold, worst-category rate, severity-weighted scores. **KILLED until built:** any "we measure tail risk" claim — today we collect the rows; the tail statistics are one derivation away and must exist before the words do.

## BT.2 — The affect build: verdict

| Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|
| `csoai/gspc-affect` on HF — 34 public + canary, PROHIBITED 19 / DISCLOSE 14 / PERMITTED 16, Art 5(1)(a)/(b) + Art 50 anchors | **REAL-pending** — not yet in HF public index (lag plausible for a minutes-old publish); falsifiable specifics supplied (keystone publish, 49 scored/34 public/15 held-out) |
| Commit `9ab14ef` on `csoai-static-deploy2`: GSPC_AXES 12→13, 3 probes, **de-hardcoded every `==12`/`/12`** incl. one hidden inside `self_test()` | **REAL-pending** — and the engineering pattern is canon-worthy: *future axes now cost one list entry, not a test sweep* |
| 83 tests green (arena 9, league 38, signal-index 16, merge-arena 20) | **REAL-pending** |
| Contamination discipline: 15-item held-out never uploaded; canary-null handling; guard clean | **Doctrine-perfect** |
| "No score exists"; old 12-axis profiles report affect as UNMEASURED | **Doctrine-perfect** — honest state, handled by code as designed |
| DISCLOSE class = axis signature | **REAL and significant** — no other GSPC bank grades Article 50 transparency duties; art5 grades PROHIBITED/PERMITTED, affect adds the disclosure duty. The axis's distinct contribution is real |

## BT.3 — Two gate deviations, named with remedies (register honesty)
1. **Counsel gate was inverted.** BN.5/BP.5 sequence: legal gold schema → counsel sign-off → *then* authoring/publishing. The bank is public before counsel. Mitigations: the anchors replicate already-published gspc-art5 label practice; no score, no conformity claim, card unsigned; no GPU/critical-path resources consumed (authored on the Mac from mined estate assets). **Remedy (canonized):** counsel's retrospective blessing — already queued for art5 — now explicitly covers gspc-affect's labels; the card stays unsigned and scoreless until the JUDGE.lock re-bolt. Register records the inversion so it does not become the norm: *precedent explains, it does not authorize.*
2. **"Axis 13" ordinal discipline.** Internal wiring (GSPC_AXES 12→13, handoff docs, code constants) = legitimate — the registry entry must exist for measurement to ever run. Public HF bank with honest unscored status = consistent with the gspc-det SPEC precedent. **Still KILLED:** any public "we have 13 axes" marketing claim until the signed card exists (BN.3). The lane's own "Gates held" section shows awareness — noted with approval.

## BT.4 — The unifying line
The fat-tail question and the affect build are the same story: **PROHIBITED is a tail concept.** Law does not grade averages — it names the events that must never happen, and the disclosures that must always happen. The middle of the distribution is where benchmarks live; the tail is where law lives. GSPC was built for the tail from the first UNMEASURED — affect just gave the tail its own bank.

## BT.5 — The sentence
**Humans have bounded bad days; models have correlated bad tails — and the entire estate is a machine for seeing the tail that averages hide.** The axis that measures manipulation, disclosure, and vulnerability now exists in code and (pending index) in public — unsigned, unquoted, and honest about it, exactly as doctrine demands.

*Part BT compiled August 12, 2026. Build REAL-pending live confirmation. Counsel inversion recorded with remedy. Tail metrics THEORY until derived. The ruler never moved — its jurisdiction awaits the re-bolt.*

**NUMBER CORRECTION (canonized):** the first lane's "PROHIBITED 19 / DISCLOSE 14 / PERMITTED 16" sums to 49 = the *full set* (34 public + 15 held-out). **Published class counts are PROHIBITED 13 / DISCLOSE 10 / PERMITTED 11 (= 34).** Same public-vs-fullset conflation caught all session (63/201, 34+18/52) — the A4 numbers registry absorbs this: every count must name its set.
**VERIFIER-BUG LESSON (canonized):** the verifying lane nearly flagged a good bank as malformed — it treated `_canary` as a presence-marker when it's a per-row boolean, miscounted 34 real items as canary, and caught itself by reading raw data instead of trusting its own tool. *A verifier's bug is as dangerous as an unverified claim.* Standing rule: probe-the-probe — any "artifact looks broken" verdict requires the same raw-data receipt as any "artifact looks perfect" claim.

## BU.2 — The Waggle Doctrine: bees solved quorum-gated collective measurement 30 million years before us
User insight: the honeybee waggle dance works WITH the fat-tail/binomial structure — and it's a true biological precedent for the entire estate architecture. Established science (background): Karl von Frisch decoded the dance (Nobel 1973); Thomas Seeley's swarm work (*Honeybee Democracy*) showed nest-site choice by **quorum** — scouts dance for candidate sites, dances decay, rival factions cross-inhibit, and only when a **quorum (~15 scouts)** gathers at one site does the swarm commit. The mapping is exact:

| Honeybee mechanism | Estate mechanism |
|---|---|
| Waggle dance encodes direction + distance + quality — calibrated, noisy, verifiable | **Signed 3KB card** — payload + provenance + Wilson interval |
| Independent scouts explore and report | **Lanes / arena citizens** |
| **Quorum threshold before the swarm commits** — nobody moves on one dance | **n≥30 + Wilson interval before quotable** — nothing sprays on one run |
| Dance decay — un-reconfirmed sources fade | **Delta notes / re-measurement cadence**; uncommitted-on-pod = ephemeral (AZ) |
| Cross-inhibition between factions dancing for rival sites | **Adversarial probes, contamination scans, Blind-Spot adjudication** |
| Scouts always exploring the unknown while foragers exploit the known | **Fat-tail sampling: sims + held-out rotations** vs board averages (BT.1) |
| No single bee decides; the threshold decides | **The judge never evolves; the quorum never moves** (JUDGE.lock) |
| Danced-but-unvisited site = not yet real | **UNMEASURED** |
| Quorum counting is literally thresholded aggregation of binary scout commitments | **Binomial mathematics — Wilson score intervals** — already the estate's quotable engine (BC/BJ) |

**The fin-tail/fat-tail fit the user spotted:** the hive runs both distributions at once — foragers exploit the known middle (fin-tail, predictable yield) while a standing fraction of scouts explores the fat tail (novel sources, catastrophic-source-collapse insurance). The estate's version is already doctrine: arenas on loops + rotating held-outs + sims sampling the tail (BD/BT). **The bees don't choose between middle and tail; they budget for both. So do we.**

## BU.3 — The boundary (register honesty, same as every convergence)
The waggle mapping is **charter-layer narrative and whitepaper framing — never measurement authority.** "Bees do it" validates nothing mathematically; the Wilson interval stands on its own. What the metaphor earns: (1) a founding-document-grade story for why quorum-gated measurement is *natural law, not bureaucracy* — 30 million years of evolution converged on our exact control flow; (2) a genuinely differentiated whitepaper frame: *"Quorum-gated measurement: the honeybee precedent for bolted-judge AI governance"* — Seeley citation, honest, nobody else has it; (3) public-language power for MEOK/Council: "no single dance moves the swarm; no single run moves the ruler." Files next to the covenant as the why, per BM.5 — the metaphor steers, the math signs.

## BU.4 — Register
| Item | Register |
|---|---|
| Axis 13 bank live, 34+canary, wired, UNMEASURED | **REAL — verified against raw file by second lane** |
| Class counts | **CORRECTED: 13/10/11 public; 19/14/16 = full set incl. held-out** |
| Verifier-bug probe-the-probe rule | **DOCTRINE — canonized** |
| Waggle/quorum mapping | **CHARTER-LAYER — narrative + whitepaper frame; never measurement authority** |
| Whitepaper: "Quorum-gated measurement" (Seeley frame) | **THEORY — queued behind B2 Delta Note #1 in the publishing pipeline** |

## BU.5 — The sentence
**Thirty million years ago the bees invented the bolted ruler: dance all you want, the swarm moves at quorum or not at all — and the affect axis just took its place on the floor under the same law.** No single dance moves the swarm; no single run moves the ruler; and no claim — not even the verifier's — survives without a probe.

*Part BU compiled August 12, 2026. Axis 13 REAL and unmeasured. Counts corrected to 13/10/11. The hive agrees with the doctrine — and the doctrine needed no permission.*

# PART BV — THE TWO-LAYER SPLIT: NON-LINEAR EYES, DETERMINISTIC RULER (2026-08-12)

## BV.0 — Scope
Lane answered "probabilistic/non-linear?" with the correct architecture: **the world-layer gets probabilistic, tail-non-linear measurement; the ruler-layer stays deterministic and simple.** Register verdict: adopted and canonized, with two register-side additions (n-hunger of tail statistics; aggregation-function pinning) and one convergence worth money.

## BV.1 — The split, canonized

| Layer | Shape | Contents | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| **World-measurement** | probabilistic + non-linear | Wilson intervals (non-linear in *p*: the √(p(1−p)/n) term) + tail aggregators: **worst-case-over-items** (max-non-linear), **CVaR** (mean of worst 5%), **correlated-failure rate** | Harm lives in the tail; the mean is a linear lie about it (BT.1) |
| **Ruler (per-item grading)** | deterministic + simple | Gold labels PROHIBITED / DISCLOSE / PERMITTED by rule; exact, signable, reproducible, adjudicable | Only a fixed ruler can be signed; a soft non-linear self-score is the child grading its own conduct (BM.5) |

**The line, verbatim-worthy:** *non-linearity belongs in how we aggregate and report measurements — never in how we grade a single item.* And the guard: **non-linear yes, opaque never** — every aggregator must be a named, computable, reproducible function anyone can re-run on the per-item rows. A vague "non-linear care score" with an opaque formula is the proofof.ai failure mode wearing math.

## BV.2 — Register addition 1: the tail is n-hungry (gate amplification)
CVaR-5% on a 34-item bank is computed from **~2 items** — the tail statistic inherits and *amplifies* the n-gate. Canonized thresholds:
- **n≥30:** mean + Wilson interval (current quotable gate) — unchanged.
- **Worst-case flag:** reportable at any n as a *binary gate signal* (any PROHIBITED-class violation: yes/no) — a point flag, never a scored claim.
- **Tail statistics (CVaR-style):** require far larger n — worst-decile at n≥100 gives 10 tail items; CVaR-5% is honest only at n≥100+. Until then: report the tail *fraction and composition* with its own uncertainty statement, never a bare number.
**Consequence: tail measurement drives bank-size requirements.** affect's 34-item bank carries the mean gate; a tail-quotable affect needs a bigger bank or pooled clean runs. This lands on the Phase-2+ roadmap, not on today's card.

## BV.3 — Register addition 2: pin the aggregation function
The JUDGE.lock hash pins the *grading*; the card must also **name the aggregation function + version** (mean / worst-case / CVaR-x% + parameters), reproducible from the published per-item rows. Otherwise the silent-number-edit sin gets a new hiding place: same rows, quietly different aggregation. Canonized: **a signed card signs three things — the gold, the rows, and the aggregator.**

## BV.4 — The convergence worth money
CVaR is the *financial* tail-risk measure — the user's "fin tail" instinct was load-bearing. **The insurance market (BE gold: Munich Re aiSure, Armilla/Chaucer, AIUC) prices tails in CVaR-shaped language natively.** A GSPC card reporting Wilson-bounded means *plus* CVaR tail statistics speaks insurer as a first language — the honey-to-money translation for tail data stops being a pitch and becomes a format. Add to BQ B1/B3: the Emotional Safety Card's tail block is the insurance-grade feature.

## BV.5 — Build status and boundary
Half-built, correctly stated by the lane: Wilson in the stack ✓; per-item rows being collected (tail visible) ✓; tail aggregators **one derivation away — THEORY until computed**. Build-before-words boundary (BT.1) restated: no "we measure tail risk" until the aggregator is named, pinned, versioned, and re-runnable on published rows. When the 13-axis board lands: compute CVaR/worst-case alongside the mean on every board — smallest possible change, turns "measurably good on average" into "measurably safe in the tail."

## BV.6 — The sentence
**Probabilistic non-linearity is how we see the harm; determinism is how we prove what we saw — and the card that signs gold, rows, and aggregator together is the first measurement artifact an insurer can read without translation.** The eyes may be as non-linear as the world demands; the ruler never moves.

*Part BV compiled August 12, 2026. Two-layer split canonized. Tail = n-hungry: mean gate n≥30, tail gate n≥100+. Aggregator pinned and versioned on every card. CVaR speaks insurer natively.*

# PART BX — INCIDENT 2026-08-12: THE PORT-UPDATE CASCADE (AND WHAT DOCTRINE SAVED)

## BX.0 — The event, reconstructed from two lane logs
Owner approved "park the board, then expose :9000" to build the universal master mesh. Sequence: (1) lane parked sibling's run_all.py outputs (21 objects: board_agi, board_asi, 12 probe collections, peritem logs) to the MinIO master `corpus/` and stopped run_all.py cleanly (owner-directed — not a doctrine breach); (2) `pod update --ports 9000/tcp,9001/tcp` descheduled the A100 (machineId: None, ~10+ min); (3) the deschedule **collaterally killed the GSPC 12-board run at minute 54** — its rows were NOT parked first; (4) the pod re-provisioned at a new endpoint (:11841, uptime 245s) **reset to base image — wiped**: boards, CF token, build dir, bench.py/tail.py copies, banks, OpenRouter key, and (if /runpod did not persist — UNVERIFIED) the MinIO master data dir itself.

## BX.1 — Loss ledger (honest accounting)

| Asset | State | Why |
|---|---|---|
| GSPC 12-board run | **KILLED at 54 min, 2/12 landed** | collateral of the restart; rows not parked |
| safety board (n=36, best 0.944 vs base 0.528) | **UNQUOTABLE — per-item rows gone** | doctrine: a result without evidence rows isn't quotable; re-measure |
| continuity board (n=33, 0.606 vs 0.394) | **rows survived on Mac — potentially quotable pending integrity check** | landed durable by accident of lane practice |
| boards-sov6 (run_all.py outputs) | parked to master `corpus/` — **possibly lost if /runpod wiped** | all three "copies" were on ONE disk (see BX.2) |
| MinIO master (evidence + corpus + mirror) | on /runpod — **persistence UNVERIFIED** | base-image reset suggests local volumes may not have traveled |
| evidence/ objects | **SURVIVE — triplicated incl. Mac** | the one real 3-copy chain |
| All code (tail.py, bench.py, severity builder, affect crosswalk, spray, JUDGE.lock) | **SURVIVES — git** | durability doctrine worked |
| HF artifacts (gspc-affect v1+v2, gspc-care 200 fix, gspc-papers scrub) | **SURVIVE** | published = durable |
| Live CF sites (Council rename, manifest P0s, article-50) | **SURVIVE — verified 2026-08-12**: meok.ai 0, councilof.ai 0, article-50 200 | independent of pod |
| **proofof.ai** | **still 8× SOV4 live (verified)** — the log's "proofof.ai fix" did NOT reach the public web | local kill ≠ public kill, again; owner gate intact and still owed |

## BX.2 — The three doctrine violations this incident named
1. **Same-host copies are not copies.** boards-sov6's "3-copy durability" was master bucket + mirror bucket on ONE disk + a Mac job that didn't cover `corpus/`. Canonized: **a copy must be an independent failure domain — different host minimum, different provider ideally. A second directory on the same disk is a symlink with extra steps.** The evidence/ chain (incl. Mac) was real; the corpus chain was theater. The lane owned this gap honestly — the register now forbids the pattern.
2. **Never mutate the production pod for convenience infra.** The original Option-C instinct (master OFF the GPU worker) was right; when CPU pods wouldn't boot, the correct fallback was *wait*, not expose the box running the estate's critical path. Canonized: **production-pod config freeze — port/volume/image mutations on the GPU worker require (a) fleet-wide job inventory, (b) full independent-host backup of ALL lanes' in-flight work, (c) owner sign-off in writing. The mesh can wait; the measurement can't.**
3. **Inventory before you touch shared infra.** Lane 1 parked the job it knew about (run_all.py) — and the restart killed the GSPC board it didn't own, unparked. The A6 fleet roster is no longer nice-to-have: **incident-justified, promoted to P0.**

## BX.3 — The doctrine that held
Everything durable-by-doctrine survived: git, HF, CF. Everything pod-local died. "Uncommitted-on-pod = ephemeral" (AZ) and "durability is where bytes land" were right — the execution had holes, and the holes now have names. Also proven live: endpoint-drift + uptime-minutes = reboot detection pattern (canonized as the standard check before concluding a pod is down vs re-provisioned).

## BX.4 — NEW DOCTRINE: durable writes from minute zero
The 54-minute kill would have cost nothing if per-item rows had streamed off-pod *as they landed*. Canonized: **every long run streams its rows to durable storage (master/git/HF) incrementally — no run may accumulate more than 5 minutes of unlanded rows.** Batch-at-end is hereby a killed pattern for anything longer than a smoke test. This is the single highest-value process fix from the incident.

## BX.5 — Severity v2 (the good news that landed durably mid-incident)
gspc-affect v2 (`4ed69ea39146e86c`, commit `1babb79`): every item carries `severity` 1–5 + `severity_basis`, anchor-derived, COUNSEL-PENDING; split byte-identical to v1; dated delta note. Doctrine-consistent throughout: severity is an **aggregation weight, never a grading input** (BV line held); the **GPD/EVT fit was correctly refused** — a badly-fit tail model is worse than none; severity was the precondition and now exists, so ξ (fin-vs-fat answered *numerically per axis*) becomes meaningful. Propagation dependency flagged: bench.py must carry item severity into per-item rows or tail stats stay frequency-only (handoff documented). On HF = survived the incident. **REAL-pending live-index confirmation.** Note: published distribution sums to the 49 full set — keep public-vs-held-out labeling explicit per BU.1.
**Counsel scope update:** the retrospective blessing now covers art5 labels + affect labels + severity basis strings.

## BX.6 — Recovery checklist (ordered, on pod re-provision)
1. Check /runpod persistence FIRST — determines whether MinIO data + parked boards survived. (Endpoint-drift pattern: check API for current port, don't assume down.)
2. If wiped: rebuild MinIO from runbook (d4c1430) + repopulate `evidence/` from the Mac third copy; boards-sov6 = sibling re-run (owned honestly as lost-unless-volume-survived).
3. Re-init CF token + build dir from git; redeploy nothing until probes pass.
4. Re-run the GSPC 12/13-board **with durable streaming writes (BX.4) and severity propagation (BX.5)** — this is now the board's v2 spec, not a re-run of v1.
5. THEN wire the mesh (3090/serverless/Oracle/Kaggle configs already bundled, commit 87883c6).
6. Owner: the volume-mount decision (sov-workspace-mtl4/merge-800) is now incident-justified, not preference — long runs cannot keep racing pod lifetimes.

## BX.7 — The sentence
**The estate lost a board and nearly lost a master because two lanes parked the job they could see and nobody parked the run they couldn't — but every byte the doctrine had already declared durable survived, which means the doctrine was right and the holes were enumerable.** Copies must be failure domains. Rows must land as they're born. And the production pod is a temple, not a toolbox.

*Part BX compiled August 12, 2026. First real incident, fully ledgered. proofof.ai still bleeding (owner gate). The board will run again — streaming, severity-aware, and parked before anyone touches a firewall rule.*

# PART BY — RECOVERY: /runpod HELD, BOTH BOARDS LANDED, ONE SELF-CAUGHT SLIP (2026-08-12)

## BY.1 — The incident's biggest unknown resolves: the persistent volume held
/runpod **persisted** across the re-provision. The MinIO master data dir and the parked boards-sov6 survived on the volume; the wipe was confined to ephemeral `/workspace` + `/root` (CF token, build dir, scp'd tooling). BX.1's "possibly lost" resolves to **SURVIVED** for everything that was on the volume. The durability hierarchy is now empirically ranked, in blood: **git/HF/CF > persistent volume > ephemeral pod disk.** The volume is the real durability primitive — and the owner-gated mount decision (BX.6 #6) is now doubly incident-justified.

## BY.2 — Both boards recovered, verified, two-machine durable
Extracted from MinIO `xl.meta` (small objects inline; `board_*.json` "files" are MinIO object directories — the recovery lane learned MinIO's storage format live), pulled to Mac, valid JSON, committed. **Mac + git + volume = three failure domains, satisfied.**

| Axis | n | Best | Base | Register |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| safety | 36 | 0.944 (sov6-agency) | 0.528 | **UPGRADED: quotable-candidate** — rows durable; publish only with Wilson interval + judge-hash context (T2 spray gate unchanged: full board + city + hash) |
| continuity | 33 | 0.606 (sov6-logic) | 0.394 | quotable-candidate, same conditions |

**Naming-drift flag (numbers-registry fodder):** the file `board_agi.json` contains the *safety* axis topped by *sov6-agency*; `board_asi.json` reads as *continuity/sov6-logic*. agi/asi/safety/continuity/agency/logic — three naming systems for two artifacts. Canonized: canonical axis names only in cards and registry; file names are not evidence of content.

## BY.3 — The slip that proves the system (praised, canonized)
The recovery lane committed "both recovered" — then checked its own receipt, found `board_agi.json` was **0 bytes** (nested SSH escaping), owned it in the open, pulled raw xl.meta to the Mac, extracted cleanly, and committed the correction. This is A5 (external-receipt rule) cutting toward the claimant again — the second self-caught verification error this session (BU.1 parser bug, BY.3 premature commit). **The pattern is now a named estate behavior: commit-claims get content-verified before they stand.** Zero trust lost; trust earned.

## BY.4 — Correct non-actions (doctrine-consistent)
- **Did NOT re-expose :9000** — the port config change that caused the cascade is in place (it persists in pod config); no further mutations. Note for the register: when MinIO re-inits, **:9000 is already publicly exposed — the BS.3 security pin (TLS, private buckets, least-priv keys) applies IMMEDIATELY, before any data flows.** The moat must not sit on an open port unencrypted.
- Did NOT thrash the extraction; stopped, relocated, did it clean. Anti-thrash discipline held under incident pressure.

## BY.5 — Owner gates now queued (updated)
1. **Volume mount on the A100** (sov-workspace-mtl4 or merge-800) — the 10 unmeasured axes need a run that survives reboots; incident-justified, top of the infra list.
2. **RunPod plugin OAuth** (`/reload-plugins` → `/mcp` → runpod → sign in) — interactive, owner-only.
3. **CF token re-install** on the pod (wiped) — then the affect front-end page unblocks (A100 lane handoff from BT).
4. Unchanged from BW Stage 1: OpenRouter, proofof.ai (still 8× SOV4 live — verified), counsel (scope now: art5 + affect labels + severity basis), Kaggle title, DSIT, positioning line.

## BY.6 — State of the measurement floor
2/12 axes measured-and-durable (safety, continuity); 10/12 await the durable re-run — whose v2 spec is now: **streaming writes from minute zero (BX.4) + severity propagation (BX.5) + fleet roster registration (BX.2) + canonical axis names (BY.2).** The board that reruns will be a better instrument than the one that died.

## BY.7 — The sentence
**The volume held, the rows came home, and the lane that recovered them caught its own false commit before the register had to — which means the estate now has two measured wins, a proven durability hierarchy, and a receipt culture that survives incidents.** Ten axes to go, and the next board writes its evidence down as it's born.

*Part BY compiled August 12, 2026. /runpod persisted. Safety + continuity durable on three failure domains. board_agi.json contains safety — names are not evidence. :9000 is already public; TLS before bytes.*

## Part CI — 2026-08-12 — THE MOAT AUDIT: what deepens, what erodes, what's missing

**Trigger:** "mine/audit all we have — what moves improve/strengthen our moats." Deliverable: `SOVOS-MOAT-AUDIT-2026-08-12.md`. Core question: what can a $50M competitor NOT copy in 12 months?

### 1. Structural finding (REAL)

**Our moats are process/reputation/asset moats, not scale moats — they deepen with time and receipts, not money.** Moat inventory: M1 law-anchored banks (DEEP — nobody else has items) · M2 statistical honesty (SHALLOW as tech — the moat is the track record, not the math) · M3 signed receipts (MEDIUM but mostly planned — planned infrastructure moats nothing) · M4 regulatory position (potential only — nothing filed) · M5 data flywheel (DOESN'T EXIST) · M6 brand · M7 distribution estate (**currently overclaiming = moat working in reverse**) · M8 doctrine-as-process-IP (uncopyable in effect, invisible until published) · **M9 independence (DEEP, permanent — only we can destroy it)** · M10 tail IP (greenfield, months-not-years window).

### 2. The fifteen strengthening moves (REAL, gate-tagged)

S1 ship contamination scans (the scan report IS the moat document) · S2 counsel blessing (banks → legally-reviewed artifacts) · S3 public held-out discipline · S4 doctrine-as-whitepapers (process IP must be prior art with our name on it) · S5 arXiv methodology (the Wilson-demanding papers must cite us) · S6 permanent corrections page (**the one asset a funded competitor cannot fake retroactively**) · S7 ship Sigsum on the flagship · **S8 file the institutional four (CO/NAIC/BEUC/CEN-CENELEC) — regulatory citation is the deepest moat available: once a rule references our method, the statute itself becomes the moat** · S9 Frontier Tail Report (first publication owns the greenfield) · S10 scoreboard as reference infrastructure · S11 hygiene = moat defense (the fastest way to erode M2/M9 is our own README) · S12 verifier wired to real cards · S13 standing adversarial bounty · S14 OSCAL/FedRAMP format lock-in · S15 per-item custody chain.

### 3. The three missing moats (REAL)

**Data flywheel** (API usage → design-partner failure data → better banks → more usage; pilots = failure data nobody else holds) · **switching costs** (GRC integrations + OSCAL + API keys; re-basing evidence is pain) · **regulatory lock-in** (S8 — the Colorado window is open today).

### 4. Threat counters (REAL)

Suite absorption → depth + speed (features copy, counsel-stamped anchors don't) · AIUC-1 bundle → independence is permanent (they cannot become independent) · EMPATH prior art → cite + differentiate · Modulos → measure the workflow, never imitate it · labs self-grading → their every ad is our ad.

### 5. Moat-maintenance rule (canon)

**Moats here are maintained, not built once.** Monthly: corrections current · scoreboard updated · zero overclaim · one institutional touch · one receipt published. A moat that isn't maintained monthly is a slide.

### CY.3 The serverless OOWM verdict (answering the lane's question)

**The architecture call is right. Green-light to build the proof — with four canon constraints.**

| Question | Verdict |
|---|---|
| API-first (OpenRouter) → serverless fallback (our models on RunPod serverless)? | **YES** — correct hierarchy: frontier APIs for best-quality pay-per-token; our own scale-to-zero endpoint as the fallback that can never rate-limit or deprecate us |
| Is serverless cheaper for fallback? | **YES for intermittent/fallback** — $1.19/hr idle pod ≈ **$857/mo** wasted "just in case"; scale-to-zero bills per-second only while a request runs. Rule bound: **serverless for intermittent/fallback, pods for constant heavy load** (A100 signing/measurement + 3090 churn stay pods) |
| Build scope | Exactly the lane's 2 pieces: (1) one sov-clan/qwen GGUF as a RunPod serverless endpoint (network volume so cold-start isn't a re-download); (2) OOWM router try-API-then-serverless + real fallback test, reporting **cold-start seconds + per-request cost** before the map marks it Live |
| Constraint 1 — signing | **The serverless worker NEVER holds the private key.** Key lives only on the pod signing node (verified design, unchanged). Serverless returns unsigned measurement → pod signing node signs. "Sign either way" = sign pod-side either way |
| Constraint 2 — keys | RunPod serverless deploy + any OpenRouter key use = **owner-gated** (RunPod OAuth/keys with Nick) |
| Constraint 3 — naming | Endpoint name + any public URL gets the public-safe scrub — no sov-*/SOVOS strings on externally visible surfaces (CY-6 canon) |
| Constraint 4 — honesty | Cold-start latency goes on the map as measured, not estimated; fallback answers carry provenance (which brain answered) so measurement chains stay truthful |

## Part CW — Defect Register Additions (2026-08-14, found during weekend card issuance)

- **CW-1 · sandbox_escape_bench inline `ci()` degenerates at p=1.0.** The module's homemade interval returns [0.0, 1.0] for 8/8 (correct Wilson: [0.676, 1.0]). Do not quote its CIs; compute Wilson with the standard formula at issuance. Fix the helper or delete it — a wrong interval on a signed artifact is worse than none.
- **CW-2 · Cross-language canonicalization mismatch: floats.** `chain.py` (Python) serializes `1.0` as `"1.0"`; the csoai.org/verify worker (JS) as `"1"` → content_id mismatch on any card body containing floats (caught live: card 205bd5b6… failed public verification; control card passed). **Standing rule: card bodies carry decimal numbers as strings** until canonicalization is language-pinned across signer and verifier. Re-issued card 20094310… verifies valid:true publicly.
- **CW-3 · Both caught by the same discipline:** probe-the-probe — the failed verification was diagnosed against a known-good control before touching the signer, and the gold bank's own `assert_gold` ran before scoring.
